{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Ju543duSXJdG1Y97YK516k","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\\section{Theoretical Analysis}\n2\t\\label{sec:theory}\n3\t\n4\tSign misattribution is the more fundamental failure mechanism because it causes active divergence, not merely slow convergence. We derive the probability of incorrect gradient direction below; full proofs appear in Appendix~\\ref{app:theory_full}.\n5\t\n6\t\\textbf{Definition 1} (Locally correct action). \\textit{An action $a_t$ is \\textbf{locally correct} if it would receive positive advantage under an oracle that observes the full episode history $h_t$ and has access to the true action-value function. Equivalently, window $k$ contains locally correct actions if replacing them (holding all other windows fixed) would decrease the probability of episode success.}\n7\t\n8\t\\textbf{Proposition 1.} \\textit{Under step-independent training with binary episode reward $R_{\\text{episode}} \\in \\{0, r_+\\}$, the probability that window $k$'s advantage $\\hat{A}_k$ has the correct sign (aligned with local action quality) approaches the base success rate $p$ as the number of rollouts $K$ per group increases.}\n9\t\n10\t\\textit{Proof sketch.} In a failed episode ($R_{\\text{episode}} = 0$), all windows receive $R_k = 0$. If any rollout in the group succeeded, $\\mu > 0$, so every window in the failed episode receives $\\hat{A}_k < 0$---including windows with locally correct actions. The probability of receiving the wrong sign in a failed episode is $1 - (1-p)^{K-1}$ (at least one group member succeeded). As $K$ grows, this approaches $1-p$, and the overall probability of correct sign approaches $p$. \\hfill$\\square$\n11\t\n12\tThe key distinction is temporal. Information loss (M1) imposes a constant ceiling determined by $h$; sign misattribution (M2) is cumulative---each wrong-sign gradient step erodes policy competence below that ceiling. A wrong-sign gradient moves the optimizer \\textit{away} from good policies; a reduced-magnitude gradient from discounting ($\\gamma^{(N-k)}$) still points toward good policies. Sign errors produce active divergence; magnitude errors merely slow convergence (Appendix~\\ref{app:sign_fundamental}).\n13\t\n14\t\\textbf{Proposition 2.}\\label{prop:correct_sign} \\textit{Let $p$ denote the episode success rate and $K$ the number of rollouts per group. Under step-independent training with binary episode reward, the fraction of gradient updates with correct sign is:}\n15\t\\begin{equation}\n16\tP(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) = \\frac{p}{1 - (1-p)^{K}}\n17\t\\label{eq:correct_conditional}\n18\t\\end{equation}\n19\t\n20\t\\textit{Derivation.} Three mutually exclusive cases determine gradient direction for a window with locally correct actions: (1)~episode succeeded (probability $p$): correct sign; (2)~episode failed but group contains a success (probability $(1{-}p)[1{-}(1{-}p)^{K-1}]$): wrong sign; (3)~entire group failed (probability $(1{-}p)^K$): zero gradient. Conditioning on non-zero gradient (Cases 1 and 2) yields Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}.\n21\t\n22\tAt $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, only 19.1\\% of gradient updates have the correct sign (Figure~\\ref{fig:theory_curve}). In the large-$K$ limit, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) \\to p$: ${\\sim}87.5\\%$ of updates for locally correct windows point in the wrong direction.\n23\t\n24\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n25\t\\centering\n26\t\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_theory_curve.pdf}\n27\t\\caption{Probability of correct gradient sign as a function of episode success rate $p$ (Proposition~2). At typical agent task success rates ($p < 0.2$, shaded), the majority of gradient updates carry incorrect sign regardless of group size $K$. At ALFWorld's baseline ($p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$), only 19.1\\% of updates are correct. The dashed diagonal shows the large-$K$ limit $P_{\\text{correct}} \\to p$.}\n28\t\\label{fig:theory_curve}\n29\t\\end{figure}\n30\t\n31\t\\paragraph{Severity scales with task difficulty.} Sign misattribution becomes \\textit{more} severe as the task gets harder (Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}): at $p{=}50\\%$, $P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update}) = 50.2\\%$---barely above random; at $p{=}5\\%$, only 5.1\\% of updates are correct. Multi-turn agent tasks with low baseline success rates are precisely where credit assignment matters most, yet where step-independent training produces the worst gradient signal. Increasing $K$ does not help: more updates occur (fewer all-fail groups), but each remains equally likely to carry the wrong sign.\n32\t\n33\t\\paragraph{Reward saturation.} When all $K$ rollouts receive the same binary reward, $\\sigma = 0$ and $\\hat{A}_k = 0$, producing zero gradient. At $p{=}12.5\\%$, $K{=}8$, the all-fail probability is $(1{-}p)^K \\approx 34.4\\%$; combined with the negligible all-success case ($p^K < 10^{-7}$), roughly one-third of batches yield no gradient at all. This compounds sign misattribution: of the ${\\sim}66\\%$ of batches that produce a gradient, only 19.1\\% have the correct sign. The effective learning rate is $0.66 \\times 0.19 \\approx 12.5\\%$ of nominal---well below the regime where convergence guarantees hold.\n34\t\n35\t\\paragraph{Cumulative divergence.} Sign misattribution produces a systematic gradient bias. Let $\\theta^*$ denote an optimal policy parameterization. At each update, the expected gradient under step-independent training is:\n36\t\\begin{equation}\n37\t\\mathbb{E}[\\nabla_\\theta \\mathcal{L}] = P_c \\cdot g_+ + (1 - P_c) \\cdot g_-\n38\t\\label{eq:biased_gradient}\n39\t\\end{equation}\n40\twhere $P_c = P(\\text{correct} \\mid \\text{update})$, $g_+$ points toward $\\theta^*$, and $g_-$ points away. Empirical measurement from training logs confirms approximately comparable per-group advantage magnitudes ($|\\hat{A}_{\\text{correct}}|/|\\hat{A}_{\\text{wrong}}| \\approx 1.32$). When $P_c < 0.5$ (which holds for $p < 0.5$; Equation~\\ref{eq:correct_conditional}), the expected gradient points weakly away from good policies. The cumulative effect of this directional bias, amplified by the M1--M2 positive feedback loop (Section~\\ref{sec:dual_mechanism}), drives progressive policy degradation: information loss (M1) caps $p$; lower $p$ increases wrong-sign frequency under M2, which further depresses $p$, producing the empirically observed collapse.\n41\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01Es5c6FoBq5ZRRLURrgwcVC","session_id":"1390f184-119e-47ed-8548-8112c5dd5d97","uuid":"6459c95b-cab0-4dac-bcba-5ecc8c544d3d","timestamp":"2026-05-23T10:59:17.117Z"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Wz4C9wEKeKyc3V7RCjDDK2","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\\\n\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nPlot h-sweep cliff figure for paper.\\\\nShows performance collapse as context window size h decreases.\\\\nInput: metrics from h-sweep runs (h={2,5,10,20,full} x 3 seeds x 15 steps)\\\\nOutput: PDF + PNG figures for paper submission\\\\n\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\nimport argparse\\\\nimport re\\\\nimport os\\\\nimport glob\\\\nimport warnings\\\\nimport numpy as np\\\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\\\nfrom collections import defaultdict\\\\n\\\\nimport matplotlib\\\\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\\\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\\\\nfrom matplotlib.ticker import MaxNLocator\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nH_VALUES = [2, 5, 10, 20, 'full']\\\\nH_LABELS = ['2', '5', '10', '20', 'full']\\\\nCOLORS = ['#d62728', '#ff7f0e', '#2ca02c', '#1f77b4', '#9467bd']\\\\nN_STEPS = 15\\\\n\\\\nVAL_SUCCESS_KEY = r\\\\\\\"val-env/AlfworldSG/success:np\\\\\\\\.float64\\\\\\\\(([^)]+)\\\\\\\\)\\\\\\\"\\\\nTRAIN_SUCCESS_KEY = r\\\\\\\"train/AlfworldSG/success:np\\\\\\\\.float64\\\\\\\\(([^)]+)\\\\\\\\)\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef parse_log_file(log_path):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Parse a tee'd log or Ray worker log for step metrics.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    steps = {}\\\\n    with open(log_path, 'r', errors='replace') as f:\\\\n        for line in f:\\\\n            # Strip ANSI codes\\\\n            line = re.sub(r'\\\\\\\\x1b\\\\\\\\[[0-9;]*m', '', line)\\\\n            # Strip Ray worker prefix like \\\\\\\"(TaskRunner pid=123456) \\\\\\\"\\\\n            line = re.sub(r'^\\\\\\\\([^)]+\\\\\\\\)\\\\\\\\s*', '', line)\\\\n            if not line.startswith('step:'):\\\\n                continue\\\\n            step_match = re.match(r'step:(\\\\\\\\d+)', line)\\\\n            if not step_match:\\\\n                continue\\\\n            step = int(step_match.group(1))\\\\n            val_match = re.search(VAL_SUCCESS_KEY, line)\\\\n            train_match = re.search(TRAIN_SUCCESS_KEY, line)\\\\n            val_success = float(val_match.group(1)) if val_match else None\\\\n            train_success = float(train_match.group(1)) if train_match else None\\\\n            steps[step] = {\\\\n                'val_success': val_success,\\\\n                'train_success': train_success,\\\\n            }\\\\n    return steps\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef find_ray_worker_logs(ray_base=\\\\\\\"/root/autodl-tmp/tmp/ray\\\\\\\"):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Find all Ray worker log files, sorted by session (newest first).\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    sessions = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(ray_base, \\\\\\\"session_*\\\\\\\")), reverse=True)\\\\n    worker_logs = []\\\\n    for session in sessions:\\\\n        logs_dir = os.path.join(session, \\\\\\\"logs\\\\\\\")\\\\n        if os.path.isdir(logs_dir):\\\\n            for f in sorted(os.listdir(logs_dir)):\\\\n                if f.startswith(\\\\\\\"worker-\\\\\\\") and f.endswith(\\\\\\\".out\\\\\\\"):\\\\n                    worker_logs.append(os.path.join(logs_dir, f))\\\\n    return worker_logs\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef load_real_data(data_dir, log_dir=None):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    Load h-sweep metrics from log files.\\\\n    \\\\n    Strategy:\\\\n    1. Look for tee'd logs at log_dir/hsweep-h{H}-seed{N}.log\\\\n    2. Fall back to scanning Ray worker logs\\\\n    \\\\n    Returns: dict[h_value][seed] = {step: {'val_success': float, ...}}\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    results = defaultdict(dict)\\\\n    \\\\n    # Strategy 1: tee'd logs\\\\n    if log_dir is None:\\\\n        log_dir = \\\\\\\"/root/runs/cost-verify-agent-rl\\\\\\\"\\\\n    \\\\n    for h in H_VALUES:\\\\n        h_str = str(h)\\\\n        for seed in range(1, 6):\\\\n            log_path = os.path.join(log_dir, f\\\\\\\"hsweep-h{h_str}-seed{seed}.log\\\\\\\")\\\\n            if os.path.exists(log_path):\\\\n                steps = parse_log_file(log_path)\\\\n                if steps:\\\\n                    results[h_str][seed] = steps\\\\n    \\\\n    # Strategy 2: Ray worker logs (scan for step: lines with val metrics)\\\\n    if not results:\\\\n        ray_logs = find_ray_worker_logs()\\\\n        for log_path in ray_logs:\\\\n            steps = parse_log_file(log_path)\\\\n            if steps:\\\\n                # Try to identify h_value from wandb run name in the log\\\\n                # For now, store as unknown\\\\n                pass\\\\n    \\\\n    return results\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef generate_demo_data():\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Generate synthetic data demonstrating the expected cliff effect.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    np.random.seed(42)\\\\n    results = {}\\\\n    \\\\n    # Expected final performance curve (cliff between h=5 and h=10)\\\\n    final_targets = {'full': 0.28, '20': 0.25, '10': 0.15, '5': 0.08, '2': 0.03}\\\\n    \\\\n    # Training dynamics: larger h learns faster and plateaus higher\\\\n    for h_str, target in f\\\\n\\\\n... Use --demo for synthetic data.\\\\\\\")\\\\n            return\\\\n    \\\\n    curves = extract_curves(results)\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"Loaded curves for h values: {list(curves.keys())}\\\\\\\")\\\\n    for h_str, c in curves.items():\\\\n        final = c['val_mean'][-1]*100 if len(c['val_mean']) > 0 else c['mean'][-1]*100\\\\n        print(f\\\\\\\"  h={h_str}: {c['n_seeds']} seeds, \\\\\\\"\\\\n              f\\\\\\\"{len(c['steps'])} steps, \\\\\\\"\\\\n              f\\\\\\\"final={final:.1f}%\\\\\\\")\\\\n    \\\\n    plot_cliff_figure(curves, args.output_dir, fmt=tuple(args.format),\\\\n                      width=args.width)\\\\n\\\\n\\\\nif __name__ == '__main__':\\\\n    main()\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.43}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_012T4Yoin8M8Hk72MGhJRaXV","session_id":"1390f184-119e-47ed-8548-8112c5dd5d97","uuid":"533de760-480e-46ea-bdf1-fcabd2aae1bd","timestamp":"2026-05-23T10:59:58.750Z"}
